These are decision thresholds, not observed effectiveness estimates. They apply only to a shared outcome: six chapters cannot be credited with teaching topics they omit. Conversely, a complete book does not automatically provide greater benefit on a narrow outcome such as fractions. The empirical question is which resource produces enough additional understanding for its additional cost.

Multilingual overlap can change the portfolio. If every useful audience were nested within the largest one and each edition delivered the same benefit, additional editions would add no unique beneficiaries. If audiences were disjoint, the displayed sums would apply. Actual allocation must account for intersecting audiences, audience-specific educational yield, register and script access, and shared production costs. Counting two editions is not automatically counting two different people.

The 25 rows with unresolved audience counts remain symbolic alternatives, not zero-need languages. For each budget, the calculation reports how large their audience-benefit contribution would need to be to improve the portfolio after paying their opportunity cost. Additional varieties, signed languages, non-academic-register English and interlanguage designs remain eligible in the wider register. None is excluded by the limits of this inherited numeric table.

The calculation appendix GLOBAL_BUDGET_SCENARIOS_v1.md and its structured JSON preserve all 775 rows, ten solved scenarios, full edition selections, budget bounds and unknown-audience entry thresholds. The figures in this section are generated from those same results and checked for exact agreement.

7.10 What direct translation evidence contributes to allocation

A translated self-study course can change participation as well as test performance. Bälter et al. (2024) compared otherwise identical Swedish and English programming courses. Among the 2,263 analyzed learners, the Swedish group had an average score 3.06 answers higher out of 42 and a test-initiation rate 13.54 percentage points higher. Here initiation means attempting at least one module-test question, not completing the course (Tables 2-3 and section 4.2).

The analysis excluded 212 language switchers. Assigning their missing outcomes every allowable value gives a registered-cohort initiation difference of 4.37 to 21.42 percentage points, still positive, but a score difference of -0.23 to 6.95 answers. These are missing-outcome bounds, not confidence intervals or a full randomized-cohort estimate; 547 assigned applicants never completed registration.

This supports familiar-language self-study as a concrete intervention to compare, without assuming a universal learning effect. For 1,000 comparable future enrollees, preserving one-quarter, one-half or all of the analyzed score difference would yield 765, 1,530 or 3,060 additional correct-answer units. These are declared transport scenarios. The denominator for efficiency is the production and delivery workload of the actual course. Correct-answer units measure aggregate performance, not distinct people helped.

Allocation should therefore compare relevant learner cohorts and particular resources, preserving participation and learning as separate outcomes. The result strengthens the case for worked exercises, feedback and accessible language in self-study packages. It does not establish a population-wide benefit multiplier for any language. The calculation appendix preserves sampling intervals, missing-outcome bounds and exact assumptions.

7.11 Shared editions under a finite budget

A new two-page supplement compares a dual-script Hindi-Urdu mathematical register with the best affordable mix of native-language editions. It retains the same screening inputs and gives every portfolio the same one-time source-preparation saving. The four declared cost decompositions produce twenty bounded optimization results, all independently checked for exact selected-cost and coefficient arithmetic.

In the low-preparation scenario, the native mix is Chinese, Hindi, Spanish, Portuguese and Indonesian; buying the bridge leaves room for Chinese, Spanish and Arabic. With no modeled pair overlap, bridge benefit above 78.29% of the normalized combined native opportunity would improve this particular portfolio. With fully nested pair audiences, 120.54% would be required. A high-shared-preparation scenario has an interior minimum of 87.41%, so its endpoints alone do not describe the full sensitivity. These are conditional benefit thresholds, not observed comprehension rates, new-learner counts or physical-compute measurements.

This is an allocation test rather than blanket credit or rejection for an interlanguage. It compares against the best affordable alternatives, not only against translating both native editions. Shared opportunity, script access, educational yield, other multilingual overlaps and native-plus-bridge hybrids remain explicit unresolved parameters or alternatives. None becomes zero need. The local source-bound calculation, selected portfolios and independent audits are in structured/HINDI_URDU_BUDGET_ENTRY_FRONTIER_v1.json and the supplement source archive. The full worldwide scope remains active.

7.12 What a focused package can afford to add

The next allocation comparison asks how much work can be spent making a focused package useful before a complete-book edition becomes more efficient. The four-page focused-package study combines actual OpenStax source structure, four direct instructional studies and exact conditional workload calculations. It does not assume that the existing source has no exercises or answers, nor that selecting fewer pages preserves the full educational benefit.

The pinned fractions source contains 108 example containers, 216 Try It notes, 19 readiness notes and 1,047 exercise nodes; 696 exercises contain a solution element. Categories are nested, not additive. These are structural counts, not a guarantee that every solution is a full explanation. Its 367 image-reference instances and links to eight outside modules identify further production work. Adding those eight directly linked modules once increases the extracted source from 41,336 to 87,655 tokens, compared with 364,270 for the full collection. This one-hop scenario is not a complete prerequisite closure or a claim that every linked module is necessary.

Let the full package cost F + V, with positive variable workload V and common setup F = fV. Let the focused package cost F + rV + E, where r is its declared source-length workload ratio and E is additional work. If alpha(E) is its final additional educational benefit relative to the full package's positive additional benefit, on the same cohort, learning outcome, alternative provision and time horizon, focused production is more efficient precisely when E/V < alpha(E)*(1+f)-f-r. Equality is a tie. When extra work changes benefit, this is a condition on the finished design, not a spending cap determined from the unimproved design.

At half the full package's benefit and setup equal to 10% of full variable work, the fractions-only base can absorb extra work of up to 33.65% of V before break-even. Including all eight directly linked modules leaves 20.94% of V for further work. Six chapters at half-benefit are already less efficient in this setup scenario; at three-quarters benefit they can absorb 24.74% of V. Ratios above one are also tested: a focused package can outperform a full book on a narrow outcome, although source length alone does not establish that result. All these benefit and setup values are declared scenarios, not fitted empirical parameters.

In the common Indonesian workload example, full-source variable production is 4.891 million token-event workload units; 10% setup adds 0.489 million. The fractions-only base is 0.555 million, leaving a 1.646 million extra-work break-even allowance at half-benefit. These units do not measure physical compute, cost in money, subscription usage or achieved learning. Chinese, Hindi, Portuguese and Filipino conversions use the same rule; all 775 inherited profile IDs remain present. No local programme accomplishment changes population need or eligibility.

The instructional literature informs concrete design rather than supplying an invented common effect. Sweller and Cooper (1985) support studying example/problem pairs in a supported short-session setting. Booth et al. (2015) found differing responses to algebra-example worksheets, rather than a significant universal effect in the larger experiment. McGuire et al. (2017) found no significant score advantage for richer fraction feedback in its selected analysis and a lower completion rate in the picture-hint group. Pitchford (2015) found useful gains from a supported Chichewa tablet package, not an isolated translation contrast or unsupported home-study effect. The supplement preserves populations, comparators, periods, selection limits and exact primary-source locators.

The practical recommendation is to compare complete-book and focused, independently usable designs on an explicit learning gap. Retain available examples, practice and answer routes; price the missing supports and delivery work; keep faithful translation separate from added pedagogy. The full book's other outcomes still count when the objective is a whole course. At global scale these alternatives must enter the fixed-budget portfolio together with audience overlap and reusable costs. Higher average benefit per workload alone does not prove which indivisible combination maximizes total benefit. The worldwide rankings and all unresolved alternatives remain part of the study.

7.13 Choosing languages and package sizes together

A fixed budget can support a better combination when it is allowed to mix focused resources and complete books. Across the twelve scenarios here, the best mixed combination improves the modelled educational-opportunity score by between zero and 22.6% over the best policy that uses one package size for every selected language. This is a conditional calculation, not a measured improvement in learning. The useful finding is that package choice and language choice must be made together.

Each language can receive one of three alternatives: the eight fractions modules with eight directly linked support modules; six selected chapters; or the complete OpenStax Prealgebra 2e source. Their extracted content contains 87,655, 173,958 and 364,270 tokens respectively. The first is a priced support scenario, not a completed prerequisite audit. The calculation allows additional per-edition work of 5% of full-book variable workload for supported fractions and 2% for six chapters. These additional-work allowances are scenarios, not measured production costs.

All three alternatives are compared on the same proposed outcome: understanding fraction magnitude, arithmetic and novel applications, assessed on a common normalized scale six weeks after the resource is offered. The currently usable educational alternative and the learner population are held consistent within each comparison. The complete book has other educational benefits; a fractions-only calculation does not value or replace its wider curriculum. Those additional outcomes require their own comparison.

Two assumptions test how much benefit a shorter resource retains. Under the lower pattern, supported fractions and six chapters deliver 35% and 70% of the full book's incremental benefit on this outcome. Under the higher pattern they deliver 65% and 90%. These are illustrative final-benefit ratios after the extra work, not estimates extracted from the instructional studies in section 7.12. The four studies guide the choice of explanations, practice and feedback, but do not identify these ratios for all languages.

Reusable preparation is charged once for the actual union of source modules. The supported-fractions package is not wholly contained in the six-chapter selection: their joint source contains 40 modules and 198,477 tokens. At the higher preparation rate it costs 396,954 workload units to prepare this joint source, compared with 523,226 if its shared modules were charged twice. Translation and review still incur their own per-edition source-reading work. No completed local translation is credited against worldwide educational need.

The table compares budgets of 5, 10 and 20 million standardized workload units. Preparation rates of zero and two additional units per distinct source token test the effect of reusable work. In the package-count column, the three numbers mean supported-fractions editions, six-chapter editions and complete-book editions. Workload is a token-event index, not money, energy or accelerator time. The final percentage compares each mixed solution with the best separately optimized policy using just one package size.

For example, with the lower benefit pattern, zero additional shared preparation and a ten-million-unit budget, the model selects the full book in Simplified Standard Written Chinese and six chapters in Spanish and French. At twenty million units it selects the full Chinese book, six chapters in Hindi, Spanish, Arabic, French and Urdu, and supported fractions in Bangla. These differences follow from the declared costs, audiences and benefit ratios; the calculation does not assume that different nationalities learn differently.

Under the higher benefit pattern at twenty million units, the selected mix is six chapters in Chinese, Hindi, Spanish and Arabic, plus supported fractions in French, Urdu, Bangla, Portuguese, Indonesian and Russian. Both preparation rates select this mix. It illustrates a conditional high-reach allocation, not a universal recommendation to give those communities only those resources. Every language had the same three choices. A different educational outcome or source-specific benefit can change the result.

The worldwide audience inputs remain unchanged for all 775 retained edition profiles. A separate set of nine secondary-school proficiency counts describes educational difficulty in particular survey populations; it is not substituted for the worldwide language audiences. In particular, the model does not shrink a researched language to a small student cohort while leaving unresearched languages with their entire population. The calculation contains 750 numerical audience estimates and 25 explicitly unknown audiences, with single-option entry thresholds for each unknown. All wider language, signed-language, register and interlanguage opportunities remain in the study; this bounded calculation is not the whole candidate universe.

Existing-supply research changes the practical alternatives worth comparing. Indonesian official student books and separate teacher guides motivate testing independent-learning companions alongside a full course. Philippine K-12 and Alternative Learning System provision motivate testing coherent exact-language, downloadable pathways and answer support. Japanese-language transition needs and Ukrainian educational disruption require their own population and resource comparisons. None of these observations measures the benefit ratio used above, proves that an entire country lacks books, or reduces eligibility. The source-bound package records and the accompanying population-and-supply crosswalk preserve those distinctions.

The objective weights each edition audience by its assumed relative benefit. To interpret it as expected score gain, multiply by an explicit common factor for the relevant fraction-learning audience and the full-book offer-level effect. Uptake is already part of an offer-level effect and must not be multiplied twice. Audience sums overlap and are not unique people. The output therefore also re-evaluates each selected mix under a nested-audience, maximum-available-benefit scenario; it does not pretend that the additive selection is optimal under that different geometry.

The calculation and its independent audit are preserved in MIXED_PACKAGE_ALLOCATION_v1.md and the accompanying structured JSON. They record every selected package, source union, budget remainder, same-size comparator and symbolic insertion. Numerical optimality bounds and exact rational recomputation check the saved results; they are not a formal proof of the floating-point optimizer. These results advance the allocation question while leaving the global educational-effect estimates and other subject portfolios open to further research.

7.14 Practical business education: production and delivery are different decisions

For adult micro-retailers, a practical business-records and decisions package is a specific vocational candidate alongside, rather than a substitute for, comprehensive accounting and corporate-finance courses. Proposed content includes explained cash records, customer-credit records, stock decisions and supplier comparisons, with worked examples, answers, reusable blank records and locally appropriate audio and text. An independently usable download should not require an ongoing teacher, account or connection. Its effect remains a disclosed scenario rather than an effect borrowed unchanged from supported instruction.

Two experiments sharpen this resource choice. Drexler, Fischer, and Schoar (2014) compared supported practical-rules and conventional-accounting courses among 1,193 loan clients in Santo Domingo. Scheduled contact time was 15 and 18 hours respectively. Both arms shared three introductory sessions and included instructors, handouts and homework. The practical course improved the business-practices index against control, but its full-sample index contrast with accounting was not statistically established (equality p = .195). Cash separation specifically favored the practical arm (p = .014). Outcomes among observed business owners and attrition-sensitive revenue findings do not identify mastery, profit gains or the effect of an unattended translation. The article does not explicitly name its delivery language.

Cole, Joshi, and Schoar (2024) tested short voice messages plus an orientation among retail microfinance clients in India and the Philippines. Hiligaynon is the stated Philippine eligibility language; Hindi and Kannada recordings are explicitly costed. Urdu was also an Indian eligibility language, but a separate Urdu recording and language-specific effect are not established by the cost paragraph. Practice-index effects support this kind of vocational offer without establishing higher regular-week profits or direct conceptual comprehension. Exact language, occupation and delivery matter; the national label Filipino would hide the Hiligaynon opportunity.

The study reports 73 minutes of Hindi audio, 85 of Kannada and 81 for the Philippine route. Historical complete-listening airtime alone cost USD 2.04, USD 2.38 and USD 14.99 respectively. These are study-era marginal airtime figures, not present quotations, full delivery costs or AI compute. At an illustrative 24 kbps, the audio payloads would be 13.14, 15.30 and 14.58 MB before headers and accompanying files. This makes an offline voice/text route concrete without pretending its uptake or effectiveness has been measured by the voice-call experiment.

The allocation objective must therefore price source preparation, translation and format production separately from per-recipient distribution or support. Compare additional understanding on the same endpoint over each cohort's currently usable alternative. An offer-level effect already incorporates uptake. Missing occupational audience counts and direct comprehension effects remain explicit parameters; neither the trial sample nor the entire language population is the vocational beneficiary count. The worldwide language baselines and rankings remain unchanged.

For example, if a download reaches five times as many distinct relevant learners as a supported route, it needs to retain more than 20% of the latter's offer-level benefit to improve total benefit, once both options fit the stated budgets. That is a conditional threshold, not a measured fivefold reach advantage. Where support is offered to people already reached by download, count only its extra benefit for those people. A scenario with download benefit 0.40, supported benefit 1.00 and support reaching half the same cohort yields 0.70, not the naive sum 0.90. Complementary outcomes require a different model.

The immediate resource recommendation is practical, independently usable business education for exact-language micro-retail audiences, with deeper accounting available for the distinct conceptual objective. Hiligaynon, Hindi and Kannada are specific source-supported leads; Urdu and other communities remain eligible with their evidence status disclosed. This is not a new worldwide language order. It adds a vocational option and a separate delivery constraint to the allocation problem. VOCATIONAL_PACKAGE_ALLOCATION_v1.md and its structured calculation preserve the source evidence, original design inference, exact thresholds and remaining uncertainties.

7.15 How audience evidence can change a compute allocation

Language populations are starting points, not automatically the audiences for every educational resource. A retail-accounting course, a secondary algebra course and a research monograph serve different immediate and future learners. The worldwide language-community and academic-English-access baseline remains visible; package-specific evidence adds to it rather than removing communities from consideration.

When only separate language and occupational totals are available, their intersection is not identified. In a common population of N people, with A meeting the language condition and B the resource-audience condition, the joint count X satisfies max(0,A+B-N) <= X <= min(A,B). These bounds follow from nonnegative cells in the two-by-two population table. A*B/N is instead an independence scenario. Disjoint, exhaustive regional detail can tighten the bounds without assuming independence.

An exact synthetic example shows the allocation consequence. A population of 100 contains 40 people meeting a language condition and 30 meeting an occupational condition, giving aggregate bounds of 0 to 30 and an independence value of 12. Two regions of 50 have respective language counts 35 and 5 and occupational counts 20 and 10. Their joint bounds sum to 5 to 25; within-region independence gives 15. Both endpoints are feasible. None of these illustrative counts represents a named community.

Give the targeted resource cost 2 and this uncertain audience; give an alternative resource cost 2 and audience 20, and a third resource cost 1 and audience 6. Assume distinct beneficiaries and a common positive reach-times-additional-benefit factor, normalized to one. With budget 3, the optimal choice changes from the alternative plus third resource at joint audience 5 to the targeted plus third resource at joint audience 25. Their additional-benefit totals are respectively 26 and 31. At joint audience 20 the combinations tie. These are arbitrary cost and normalized benefit units, not measured compute or observed learning. Missing joint evidence therefore identifies a possible allocation reversal, not an ineligible language. A zero lower bound is not zero need.

Primary survey documentation supplies routes to better estimates. IHDS-II records respondent-associated mother tongue and business decision-maker IDs that can be linked to its person roster; this is not individual academic-language proficiency. The Philippine 2020 census dictionary similarly links household home-language and work-industry concepts. Neither access check obtained analysis-ready joint records, and no national entrepreneur or learner count is claimed. These historical routes also require explicit updating assumptions before any present-day use (NCAER & University of Maryland, n.d.; Desai & Vanneman, 2018; Philippine Statistics Authority, 2024a, 2024b).

Weights need the same care as audience definitions. An individual cohort share sum(w*I)/sum(w) is unchanged when every survey weight is multiplied by a constant, whereas the numerator changes. Thus an absolute population count requires a documented expansion scale or a matched population calibration. Household-size weights must not be applied again to each person. Repeated business slots must not count the same learner repeatedly; missing language or work information remains unresolved rather than false. The IHDS guidance supports individual cross-sectional WT and documents household-size multiplication in INDWT (Lazaroff, n.d.; India Human Development Survey, n.d.).

The accompanying calculation implements exact compatible-count bounds, stratified alternatives, budget selections and weighted-share checks. Its 26 checks cover 819 small marginal-count combinations and 3,025 pairs of strata; these validate the stated arithmetic, not real population estimates or survey-key and weight provenance. Fractional independence scenarios are distinguished from actual integer population tables. The decision rule for further research is to retain an explicit reference scenario and possible winning alternatives while replacing assumptions with comparable joint evidence. This applies to language, academic register, educational stage, occupation, device access and interlanguage overlap. Additional learning and standardized physical compute remain distinct quantities to estimate. The full method and source passages are recorded in AUDIENCE_JOINT_ALLOCATION_v1.md and structured/AUDIENCE_JOINT_ALLOCATION_v1.json.

8. Worldwide reach and common-package allocation checkpoint

Status: ongoing research checkpoint. This restores the quantitative allocation chain; it is not the final package-specific allocation result.

What is being answered

The research question remains: how should AI compute be distributed so that educational-material availability produces the greatest additional functional understanding for as many people as possible?

The answer needs more than one list. The first table estimates potential reach. The second asks how that reach changes when every target receives exactly the same one 100,000-source-token, text-only educational package translated and checked to the independent-context-plus-deterministic-qa-v2 target. Later target-specific tables must replace the held-equal assumptions with evidence about the actual stage, subject, baseline supply, learning effect, delivery conditions, accessibility work, reusable setup, and overlapping beneficiaries.

Profile IDs are never ranks. Ordinal positions appear only in columns explicitly named Reach position or Common-package proxy position.

Result 1: potential reach under one common missing-data scenario

The point multiplier is 89.57%; the displayed sensitivity range is 70%–99%. Missing comparable academic-register evidence keeps the common scenario instead of deleting or lowering a target. Chinese in the Simplified-script profile is position 1. Indonesian is position 9 after retaining the direct 2022 BPS communicative-ability population as its point population. Russian is position 10; Ukrainian is position 62 in the pinned CLDR reach layer. Those are potential-reach positions, not judgments about people or final funding positions.

Result 2: the same package divided by standardized token-event workload

This is the first quantitative compute-allocation bridge. It holds the package and nominal QA target fixed, uses a measured language/script workload where a defensible binding exists, and otherwise uses the identical language-blind envelope. Missing tokenization evidence therefore cannot remove a row. The displayed unit is potential readers per one million fresh-equivalent token events.

It is a conditional proxy, not the final efficiency result: it assumes equal incremental educational effect and equal delivery. Fresh-equivalent token events are not physical compute, time, energy, money, or quality.
